          "text": "## 3. Results and discussion\n## 3.1. Reductive leaching\nFig. 5 shows the leaching behavior of cathodic active materials in 1 M nitric acid solution when other conditions were fixed at 20 g/L S/L ratio and 75 ^∘C. In the absence of a reducing agent, the leaching efficiencies of the cobalt and lithium levelled off within 30 min with low extractions of 40% Co and 75% Li. To increase the leaching efficiency, 0.8 vol.% hydrogen peroxide was added as a reducing agent (Zhang et al., 1998). The extractions of both metals were improved to about 85% by the reductive leaching as shown in the figure. It seems that Co ^2 ^+ (cobaltous) is more readily dissolved than Co ^3+ (cobaltic).\nTo obtain the optimum conditions for the reductive leaching of LiCoO2, several sets of leaching experiments were made under different conditions, i.e. various HNO3 concentrations, S/L ratio, temperature, and H2O2 amount added. Fig. 6 shows the effect of HNO3 concentration on the leaching of LiCoO2 at 20 g/L S/L ratio, 75 ^∘ ^,, 0.8 vol.% H2O2,, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li increased with increasing HNO3 concentration. More than 80% of Co and Li was extracted within 30 min above 1 M HNO3\nThe leaching behavior with different S/L ratios is presented in Fig. 7 at a fixed condition of 1 M HNO3, 75 ^∘C, 0.8 vol.% H2O2, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li decreased with increasing S/L ratio. Extractions of 90% of Co and Li were achieved within 30 min when S/L ratio was lower than 20 g/L for H2O2 addition of 0.8 vol.%.\nFig. 8 shows the effect of temperature on the leaching at 10 g/L S/L ratio, 1 M HNO3, 1.7 vol.% H2O2, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li increased with increasing temperature. The extent of Co extraction was close to 99% at 75 ^∘C,, 75% at 50 ^∘C and 30% at 25 ^∘C, respectively, and that of Li was similar to Co extraction.\nFig. 9 shows the effect of H2O2 concentration on the leaching at 20 g/L S/L ratio, 1 M HNO3, 75 ^, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li increased with increasing H2O2 addition. The amount of 1.7 vol.% H2O2 was sufficient for the complete dissolution of Co and Li.",

Reductive leaching\nFig. 5 shows the leaching behavior of cathodic active materials in 1 M nitric acid solution when other conditions were fixed at 20 g/L S/L ratio and 75 ^∘C. In the absence of a reducing agent, the leaching efficiencies of the cobalt and lithium levelled off within 30 min with low extractions of 40% Co and 75% Li. Fig. 6 shows the effect of HNO3 concentration on the leaching of LiCoO2 at 20 g/L S/L ratio, 75 ^∘ ^,, 0.8 vol.% H2O2,, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li decreased with increasing S/L ratio. Extractions of 90% of Co and Li were achieved within 30 min when S/L ratio was lower than 20 g/L for H2O2 addition of 0.8 vol.%.\nFig. 8 shows the effect of temperature on the leaching at 10 g/L S/L ratio, 1 M HNO3, 1.7 vol.% H2O2, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li increased with increasing temperature. Reductive leaching\nFig. 5 shows the leaching behavior of cathodic active materials in 1 M nitric acid solution when other conditions were fixed at 20 g/L S/L ratio and 75 ^∘C. In the absence of a reducing agent, the leaching efficiencies of the cobalt and lithium levelled off within 30 min with low extractions of 40% Co and 75% Li. To increase the leaching efficiency, 0.8 vol.% hydrogen peroxide was added as a reducing agent (Zhang et al., 1998). It seems that Co ^2 ^+ (cobaltous) is more readily dissolved than Co ^3+ (cobaltic).\nTo obtain the optimum conditions for the reductive leaching of LiCoO2, several sets of leaching experiments were made under different conditions, i.e. various HNO3 concentrations, S/L ratio, temperature, and H2O2 amount added. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li increased with increasing HNO3 concentration. More than 80% of Co and Li was extracted within 30 min above 1 M HNO3\nThe leaching behavior with different S/L ratios is presented in Fig. 7 at a fixed condition of 1 M HNO3, 75 ^∘C, 0.8 vol.% H2O2, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li decreased with increasing S/L ratio. Extractions of 90% of Co and Li were achieved within 30 min when S/L ratio was lower than 20 g/L for H2O2 addition of 0.8 vol.%.\nFig. 8 shows the effect of temperature on the leaching at 10 g/L S/L ratio, 1 M HNO3, 1.7 vol.% H2O2, and 30 min. The leaching efficiency of Co and Li increased with increasing temperature. The extent of Co extraction was close to 99% at 75 ^∘C,, 75% at 50 ^∘C and 30% at 25 ^∘C, respectively, and that of Li was similar to Co extraction.\nFig. 9 shows the effect of H2O2 concentration on the leaching at 20 g/L S/L ratio, 1 M HNO3, 75 ^, and 30 min.           "text": "## 3.2. Kinetic considerations\nAssuming that leaching of Co and Li has no interdependence and the dissolution rate is inversely proportional to their respective ionic concentrations (CCo and CLi), the square of Ci has a linear relationship with respect to reaction time as described by the following equation, where Ci represents the concentration of species i in the leaching solution(mol cm ^-3), k is the apparent rate constant (mol ^2 cm ^-6 s ^-1), and t is time (s). Fig. 10 is the plot of square of C vs. time, which is drawn from the effect of temperature (Fig. 8). From the linearity observed as shown in the case of Co (Fig. 10 (a)) and Li (Fig. 10(b)), the assumptions could be confirmed. Therefore, the dissolution rate of Co and Li was inversely proportional to their respective ionic concentrations. The apparent activation energy of Co and Li was calculated from the Arrhenius plot (Fig. 11) to be 12.5 and 11.4 kcal/mol, respectively. Therefore, the dissolution of LiCoO2 seems to be controlled by surface chemical reaction (Han and Lawson, 1980).",
